**Layout**

Two-column layout on a white field: a narrow left text column (x 3.5% to about 40%) holding the title-to-bullets copy, and a right chart area (x 45.3% to 94.1%) holding one wide Liquidity Ratios chart above two side-by-side chart panels (Personal Deposits and Business & Government). The title sits top-left at y 12.6% above the left column, starting at x 3.5%, with the TD logo in the top-right corner (x 95.0%, y 3.9%). A thin vertical grey divider runs at about x 43% from y 19% to y 92.8% separating the columns. Footer sits bottom-left at y 92.7%: page number 34 at x 3.9%, followed by an em dash and the text TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026 at x 5.7%. Left and right margins are about 3.5% and 5.9%.

**Visuals**

Chart 1, Liquidity Ratios (top right, x 46 to 94%, y 24 to 51%): line chart with a green header bar titled Liquidity Ratios with superscript 5. Categories on the x-axis: Q2'24, Q3'24, Q4'24, Q1'25, Q2'25, Q3'25, Q4'25, Q1'26. No y-axis is printed; a thin light grey horizontal baseline sits under the points. Series 1, Liquidity Coverage Ratio (LCR): pale green line (about #CDE8A8) with round markers, labelled values 126%, 129%, 138%, 141%, 141%, 138%, 130%, 137%. Series 2, Net Stable Funding Ratio (NSFR): dark green line (about #006400) with round markers, labelled values 114%, 115%, 116%, 116%, 119%, 117%, 117%, 115%. Legend centred below the axis: pale green line-with-marker symbol followed by Liquidity Coverage Ratio (LCR); dark green line-with-marker symbol followed by Net Stable Funding Ratio (NSFR). No highlighted points.

Chart 2, Personal Deposits ($B) (bottom centre, x 45 to 69%, y 58 to 94%): line chart with a green header bar. Y-axis labels 300, 500, 700 (in $B), range about 300 to 700, with light grey gridlines at 500 and 700 and the 300 baseline. X-axis quarterly labels Q3'21, Q1'22, Q3'22, Q1'23, Q3'23, Q1'24, Q3'24, Q1'25, Q3'25, Q1'26, rotated about 45 degrees. Series Personal Deposits (solid, bright green about #00A000, thick): values not printed; about 600 at Q3'21, rising to about 640 around Q3'22, dipping to about 560 near Q1'23, about 600 around Q1'24, peaking near 650 around Q1'25, then about 630 at Q1'26. Series Personal Deposits (Ex. Schwab) (dashed, pale green about #A8D5A2): values not printed; about 450 at Q3'21, rising to about 510 by Q1'24, about 540 near Q1'25, about 540 at Q1'26. Two dashed grey trend arrows: upper arrow across the solid series and lower arrow across the dashed series, both pointing right. Labels 0.10% CAGR (above the dashed series, mid-chart) and 4.32% CAGR (below the dashed series, lower-left area). Legend below the x-axis: solid green line labelled Personal Deposits; dashed pale green line labelled Personal Deposits (Ex. Schwab). No highlighted points.

Chart 3, Business & Government ($B) (bottom right, x 70.5 to 94.5%, y 58 to 94%): line chart with a green header bar. Y-axis labels 200, 300, 400, 500 ($B), range 200 to 500, gridlines at 300, 400, 500. X-axis quarterly labels Q3'21, Q1'22, Q3'22, Q1'23, Q3'23, Q1'24, Q3'24, Q1'25, Q3'25, Q1'26, rotated. Series (solid bright green about #00B33C, thin): values not printed; about 385 at Q3'21, about 395 in 2022, dipping to about 365 in early 2023, about 380 in mid 2024, rising to about 405 in 2025, peaking about 415 in late 2025, then about 410 at Q1'26. Dashed grey trend arrow below the series pointing right, labelled 1.34% CAGR. No legend, no highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean corporate investor-deck style on white #FFFFFF. Title in bold green #008A00, body copy and chart text in dark green #03522C, section header bars filled #03522C with white #FFFFFF bold text. Accent colours are greens: LCR light green about #CDE8A8, NSFR dark green about #006400, personal deposits about #00A000, business series about #00B33C, grey trend lines about #888888, divider grey about #A0A0A0. Typeface is Arial (Arial Bold for titles, headers, key statements and CAGR labels; Arial regular for bullets, chart labels and footer). Title size about 5.2% of slide height, body text about 2.6%, chart labels about 1.67%, footer and legend text about 1.3 to 1.5%. Bullets are small filled square glyphs (Wingdings) in dark green. Lines are thin and unshaded; no corner radii, shadows or gradients. Bars are plain rectangles with square corners.

**Text**

Strong Credit Ratings / Issuer Ratings¹ / Moody's / S&P / DBRS / Fitch / Senior Debt² Ratings / A2 / A- / AA (low) / AA- / Outlook / Stable / Stable / Stable / Negative / Moody's / S&P / Fitch / Senior Debt² / HoldCo³ Rating / A1 / A2 / A3 / TD / Canadian / Peers⁴ / U.S. / Peers⁵ / AA- / A+ / A / TD / Canadian / Peers⁴ / U.S. / Peers⁵ / BBB+ / A- / A / TD / Canadian / Peers⁴ / U.S. / Peers⁵ / NVCC Tier 2 Subordinated / Debt Ratings¹ / A3 / Baa1 / TD / Canadian Peers⁴ / A / A- / BBB+ / TD / Canadian Peers⁴ / A / A- / TD / Canadian Peers⁴ / Additional Tier 1 NVCC LRCN / and Preferred Share Ratings¹ / Baa2 / Baa3 / TD / Canadian Peers⁴ / BBB / BBB- / TD / Canadian Peers⁴ / BBB+ / BBB / TD / Canadian Peers⁴ / 35 — TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026

**Layout**

Single 16:9 slide with a left-aligned bold title at top-left (x 3.5%, y 12.6%) and a small TD logo in the top-right corner (x 95%, y 3.9%). A full-width issuer ratings table (x 3.5% to 92.4%, y 23.2% to 34.7%) sits below the subtitle 'Issuer Ratings¹' at x 3.7%, y 19.7%. Below the table is a 4-column grid of chart panels: a row-label column at left (x 4% to 25%) and three rating-agency columns (Moody's, S&P, Fitch) with headers at y 37.4%; three horizontal rows of panels are separated by thin grey rules at about y 59.7% and y 77.0%. The footer page number '35' sits at x 3.9%, y 92.7%, followed by the footer text; there is no slide-number graphic beyond this, and margins are about 3.5% left and 7.5% right.

**Visuals**

Table: columns are an unlabelled row-header column, Moody's, S&P, DBRS, Fitch. Rows: header row (dark green cells with white bold labels Moody's, S&P, DBRS, Fitch); 'Senior Debt² Ratings' row with values A2, A-, AA (low), AA- ; 'Outlook' row with values Stable, Stable, Stable, Negative. Nine chart panels, each a floating range (floating-bar) chart with a y-axis of rating-notch labels, no numeric axis units, dashed light grey horizontal gridlines at each notch, a dark green thin bar for TD and a light green wide bar for peer ranges, and category labels below: TD (bold green), Canadian Peers⁴ and U.S. Peers⁵ (regular dark green). Panel 1 Moody's Senior Debt/HoldCo: axis A1, A2, A3; TD bar about A2 (single notch, thin, about 0.5% of slide height); Canadian Peers light bar about A1 to A2; U.S. Peers light bar about A1 to A3. Panel 2 S&P Senior: axis A, A-, BBB+; TD bar about A-; Canadian Peers about A- to A; U.S. Peers about BBB+ to A. Panel 3 Fitch Senior: axis AA-, A+, A; TD bar about AA-; Canadian Peers about AA- (thin); U.S. Peers about A to AA-. Panel 4 Moody's NVCC Tier 2: axis A3 (top), Baa1 (bottom); TD bar about A3; Canadian Peers light bar about Baa1 to A3. Panel 5 S&P NVCC Tier 2: axis A, A-, BBB+; TD bar about BBB+; Canadian Peers about BBB+ to A. Panel 6 Fitch NVCC Tier 2: axis A (top), A- (bottom); TD bar about A; Canadian Peers thin light bar about A. Panel 7 Moody's Additional Tier 1: axis Baa2 (top), Baa3 (bottom); TD bar about Baa2; Canadian Peers light bar about Baa2 to Baa3. Panel 8 S&P Additional Tier 1: axis BBB (top), BBB- (bottom); TD bar about BBB-; Canadian Peers light bar about BBB- to BBB. Panel 9 Fitch Additional Tier 1: axis BBB+ (top), BBB (bottom); TD bar about BBB+ to BBB+ (thin); Canadian Peers thin light bar about BBB+. No legend. No highlighted points beyond the TD bars in dark green. Logo: TD square logo at top right.

**Design**

Background: Plain white (#FFFFFF) full-slide background, no gradient or photograph.

**Layout**

Single-column layout with the title top-left at about 3.5% left margin, and the TD logo boxed at the top-right corner (x 95%). A subtitle and three-level bullet list sit beneath the title in the upper-left, followed by a chart heading and a full-width column chart spanning about 3.1% to 95% horizontally, with the legend centred under the x-axis. The footer (page number 36 and the document line) sits bottom-left at about 92.7% height.

**Visuals**

Stacked column chart titled Maturity Profile (C$B), with superscript notes 1,2, subtitle To first par redemption date. Y axis: C$ billions, linear, 0 to 80 in steps of 10, with light grey horizontal gridlines. X axis: categories 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025, YTD, 2026, 2027, 2028, 2029, 2030. A grey dashed vertical divider at about x 53% separates the Issued section (left, labelled Issued in underlined bold dark green) from the Maturing section (right, labelled Maturing). Series in stacking order from bottom: Tier 1 (#48C686 mint green, footnote 3), Subordinated Debt (#008A00 dark green), Senior Debt (#92D050 light green), MBS (#555555 dark grey), Covered Bond (#00B050 bright green), ABS (#BFBFBF light grey). No data labels are printed on the bars; values below are estimates marked about. Approximate stacked totals: 2021 about 25 (Tier 1 about 1.8, Senior Debt about 20.5, MBS about 2, ABS about 0.5); 2022 about 68.5 (Tier 1 about 5.8, Senior Debt about 43.5, MBS about 2, Covered Bond about 17, ABS about 0.5); 2023 about 51.5 (Senior Debt about 27, MBS about 1.5, Covered Bond about 22.5, ABS about 0.5); 2024 about 50 (Tier 1 about 1.9, Subordinated Debt about 3.2, Senior Debt about 13.5, MBS about 2.5, Covered Bond about 26.5, ABS about 2.5); 2025 about 41.8 (Tier 1 about 2, Subordinated Debt about 2, Senior Debt about 27, MBS about 4.5, Covered Bond about 5, ABS about 1.3); YTD about 10 (Senior Debt about 6.3, MBS about 1, Covered Bond about 2.3, no visible Tier 1); 2026 about 46 (Tier 1 about 1.8, Subordinated Debt about 3, Senior Debt about 19, MBS about 2, Covered Bond about 19, ABS about 1); 2027 about 60 (Tier 1 about 6, Subordinated Debt about 1.5, Senior Debt about 25, MBS about 2, Covered Bond about 24, ABS about 1.5); 2028 about 32 (Senior Debt about 21, MBS about 2, Covered Bond about 9, no visible Tier 1 or ABS); 2029 about 21 (Tier 1 about 1.8, Subordinated Debt about 3, Senior Debt about 6.5, MBS about 1.5, Covered Bond about 8); 2030 about 20.5 (Tier 1 about 1.8, Subordinated Debt about 1, Senior Debt about 13, MBS about 2, Covered Bond about 2.5). Legend horizontal below the x axis, in order Tier 1 3, Subordinated Debt, Senior Debt, MBS, Covered Bond, ABS, each with a small square swatch in the series colour. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean corporate style on white. Title in bold green #008A00. Subtitle and bullet text in dark green #03522C. Chart axis and category labels in near-black #1C1C1C. Accent series colours: #48C686, #008A00, #92D050, #555555, #00B050, #BFBFBF. Typeface is Arial (Arial-BoldMT for headings and labels, ArialMT for body and axis text), sans-serif, regular and bold weights. Title is about 5.2% of slide height; subtitle and chart heading about 3.0%; bullets, axis labels and legend about 2.2% to 1.8%; footer about 1.5%. Thin grey gridlines and axis lines, a grey dashed vertical divider, square bullets. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 38

**Text**

Wholesale Term Debt Composition / Funding Strategy / ▪ Wholesale term funding through diversified sources across domestic and international markets / ▪ Manage to a stable funding profile that emphasizes funding assets and contingencies to the appropriate term / ▪ Well-established C$100B Legislative Covered Bond Program is an important pillar in global funding strategy / ▪ Programmatic issuance in the U.S. market for the established ABS program backed by Canadian credit card receivables / ▪ Broadening of investor base through currency, tenor and structure diversification / ▪ Recent transactions: / – GBP 1.25B 5Y Float Covered Bond / – CAD 3.00B 5Y Fixed Senior MTN / – USD 1.60B 2Y Fixed/Float Senior MTN / – USD 0.90B 5Y Fixed Senior MTN / By Currency1,2 / By Term1,2 / Wholesale Term Debt1,2 / AUD $6.3B (2.9%) / GBP $10.2B (4.8%) / Mortgage Securitization3 $15.2B (7.1%) / Term Asset Backed Securities $3.9B (1.8%) / Covered Bonds $72.6B (33.9%) / 4 to 5 Year $79.8B (37.3%) / 5 to 7 Year $36.7B (17.1%) / EUR $63.1B (29.5%) / Capital4 $22.3B (10.4%) / USD $69.6B (32.5%) / > 7 Year $34.2B (16.0%) / CAD $61.1B (28.5%) / < 4 Year $63.5B (29.6%) / Senior Unsecured MTN $100.2B (46.8%) / Other $3.9B (1.8%) / 37 — TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026

**Layout**

Single white slide with a left-aligned title and subtitle block at the top left, a four-line-plus bullet list beneath, then three equal-height column panels across the lower half, each with a dark green header bar above a pie chart. The TD logo sits top right; the page number 37 and footer line sit bottom left, with a left margin of about 3.5 percent and a right margin of about 3 percent.

**Visuals**

Three pie charts side by side, each with direct labels and no legend. (1) By Currency: segments clockwise from the top are AUD $6.3B (2.9%) light green #AED99E, GBP $10.2B (4.8%) mid green #86CA72, USD $69.6B (32.5%) bright green #00B624, Other $3.9B (1.8%) pale grey-green #E0EBE7 with leader line, CAD $61.1B (28.5%) dark green #163D22, EUR $63.1B (29.5%) pale green #E6F1E0. (2) By Term: 4 to 5 Year $79.8B (37.3%) dark green #163D22, 5 to 7 Year $36.7B (17.1%) pale green #E6F1E0, > 7 Year $34.2B (16.0%) mid green #86CA72, < 4 Year $63.5B (29.6%) bright green #00B624. (3) Wholesale Term Debt: Covered Bonds $72.6B (33.9%) bright green #00B624, Mortgage Securitization3 $15.2B (7.1%) light green #AED99E, Capital4 $22.3B (10.4%) mid green #86CA72, Senior Unsecured MTN $100.2B (46.8%) dark green #163D22, Term Asset Backed Securities $3.9B (1.8%) pale #E0EBE7 with leader line. No values are estimated; all printed. Segments have thin white separators, no highlighted points. The TD logo is a green square with a white TD mark.

### Slide 39

**Review notes**

- What would change the score: I would split or trim the densest pages (slides 12, 36, 39, 41, 43) and move the long forward-looking text on slide 2 and the endnotes on slides 49 to 54 into a handout, with the first slide's empty lower area and the sparse slide 55 tidied.

**Text**

TD Global Legislative Covered Bond Program
Key Highlights
Covered Bond Collateral
Canadian residential real estate property with no more than 4 residential units
Uninsured conventional first lien assets with original loan to value ratio that is 80% or less
Housing Market Risks
Latest property valuation shall be adjusted at least quarterly to account for subsequent price adjustments using the Indexation Methodology
Tests and
Credit Enhancements
Asset Coverage Test
Asset Percentage
Amortization Test
Reserve Fund
Valuation Calculation
Prematurity Liquidity
Level of Overcollateralization
OSFI limit
Required Ratings and Ratings
Triggers
No less than one Rating Agency must at all times have current ratings assigned to bonds outstanding
All Ratings Triggers must be set for:
Replacement of other Counterparties
Establishment of the Reserve Fund
Pre-maturity ratings
Permitted cash commingling period
Interest Rate and Currency Risk
Management of interest rate and currency risk:
Interest rate swap
Covered bond swaps
Ongoing Disclosure Requirements
Monthly investor reports shall be posted on the program website
Plain disclosure of material facts in the Public Offering Document
Audit and Compliance
Annual specified auditing procedures performed by a qualified cover pool monitor
Deliver an Annual Compliance Certificate to the Canada Mortgage and Housing Corporation ("CMHC")
38
— TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026

**Layout**

Single slide with a title band across the top-left above a full-width table, a small TD logo in the top-right corner, and a footer at bottom-left. The title sits at about 12.6% from the top with a left margin of about 3.5%. The table spans about 3.2% to 96.2% horizontally and runs from about 19.5% to 86.0% vertically. It has a dark green header row, then six body rows; the left label column is about 19.2% wide and the content column is about 73.7% wide. The second row of the Tests and Credit Enhancements cell is split into two columns at about 51.3%. Content is left-aligned. The footer carries a page number and a dash-prefixed label on the left.

**Visuals**

None apart from the TD logo in the top-right corner and the table itself. The table is a structured grid with 7 rows: a header row 'Key Highlights' spanning the full width, then six body rows with label cells on the left and bullet content on the right. The Tests and Credit Enhancements row has two bullet columns: left column 'Asset Coverage Test', 'Amortization Test', 'Valuation Calculation', 'Level of Overcollateralization'; right column 'Asset Percentage', 'Reserve Fund', 'Prematurity Liquidity', 'OSFI limit'. The logo is a solid green square (#00A300-like) with a white stylised 'TD' monogram, roughly square, 42 by 37 pixels, not cropped.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background with no gradient or texture. Title in bold Arial (grotesque sans-serif) in green #008A00, about 5.2% of slide height, capitals and lowercase in title case. Table header bar fill #03522C with white bold Arial text about 2.2% of slide height. Label column cells fill #E6F1E0 (pale green) with bold Arial text in #03522C at about 1.8% of slide height. Content cells fill #FFFFFF with regular Arial body text in #03522C at about 1.8% of slide height. Thin light grey (#D0D0D0-like) 0.75pt hairline borders separate every cell, with square corners and no shadows. Bullets are small filled squares in #03522C (Wingdings), sub-bullets are en dashes in #03522C. Footer text is about 1.5% of slide height, regular Arial, page number #1C1C1C and footer label #000000. No shadows, no rounded corners.

### Slide 40

**Text**

TD Global Legislative Covered Bond Program
Highlights
▪ Pool comprises 100% uninsured 1st lien Canadian Residential Mortgages originated by TD
▪ All loans have original LTVs of 80% or lower. Current weighted average LTV is 56.55%1
▪ The weighted average of non-zero credit scores is 781
▪ C$100B program; C$73B aggregate principal outstanding2; total pool C$109B
▪ TD's Covered Bond Ratio is 3.64% (5.5% limit)
▪ Strong credit ratings; Aaa/ AAA / AAA by Moody's / DBRS / Fitch respectively3
▪ Covered Bond Label affiliate4 reporting using the Harmonized Transparency Template; complies with minimum disclosure/transparency standards per Article 14 of the EU Covered Bond Directive

39 — TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026

**Layout**

Three-column-by-two-zone layout on a white page: a left text column carries the title, the Highlights heading and six bullets across the top, with three pie charts beneath it in the lower-left, while a narrow right column stacks three bar charts top to bottom. The title sits top-left at about 12.6% from the top and aligns with the left margin at 3.5%. The TD logo is in the top-right corner, the footer runs bottom-left at about 92.7% from the top, and the slide number 39 precedes the footer rule text.

**Visuals**

Six charts in total, all in green shades. (1) Pie chart titled Issuances, set in a dark green header box; slices clockwise from 12 o'clock: EUR 55% (dark green #163D22), USD 22% (bright green #00B624), GBP 10% (mid green #86CA72), AUD 6% (light green #AED99E), CAD 6% (pale green #E6F1E0), CHF 2% (grey, thin slice at top). Labels sit outside small slices with leader lines for CAD and CHF. (2) Pie chart titled Provincial Distribution in a dark green header box; slices clockwise from 12 o'clock: Ontario 58% (#163D22), Prairies 10% (#00B624), British Columbia 24% (#86CA72), Quebec 7% (#AED99E), Atlantic 2% (#E6F1E0 with leader line). (3) Pie chart titled Interest Rate Types in a dark green header box; Fixed 65% (dark #163D22, clockwise from 12 o'clock to about 7 o'clock) and Variable 35% (bright #00B624, left side). No axes or legend; labels are printed on the slices. (4) Vertical bar chart titled Remaining Term, x-axis in months (categories <6, 6-11, 12-23, 24-35, 36-41, 42-47, 48-59, >=60, labels angled), y-axis unlabelled with no numeric scale; bars in #00B624 with values printed above: 12%, 19%, 33% (highest), 16%, 3%, 5%, 11%, 1%. (5) Vertical bar chart titled Current LTV with categories <20, 20.01-30.00, 30.01-40.00, 40.01-50.00, 50.01-55.00, 55.01-60.00, 60.01-65.00, 65.01-70.00, 70.01-75.00, 75.01-80.00, >80.00; bars #00B624 with values 3%, 7%, 11%, 15% (highest), 9%, 8%, 8%, 8%, 9%, 10%, 11%. (6) Vertical bar chart titled Credit Score with categories <599, 600-650, 651-700, 701-750, 751-800, >800; bars #00B624 with values 1%, 1%, 5%, 13%, 36%, 44% (highest). Baseline axis lines are grey, no gridlines, no y-axis numbers.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean white corporate slide with a green palette. Title in bold Arial-style sans-serif (Helvetica-like grotesque), colour #008A00, about 5.2% of slide height. Heading Highlights and chart titles in bold sans-serif, #03522C and #1A5336, about 2.2% of slide height. Body bullets in regular Arial, #03522C, about 2.2% of slide height. Chart value labels in regular Arial, black #000000, about 1.7% of slide height; category labels in dark green #03522C, about 1.8%. Header boxes are solid #03522C rectangles with bold white Arial text, square corners. Bullet glyphs are small Wingdings squares in #03522C. Pie slices separated by thin white lines. Footer text in Arial, about 1.5% of slide height, black. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

**Text**

Bail-in Overview
Scope of Bail-in
In Scope Liabilities. Senior unsecured long-term debt (original term to maturity of 400 or more days) that
is tradable and transferable (has a CUSIP, ISIN or other similar identification) and issued on or after
September 23, 2018¹. Unlike other jurisdictions, Canadian D-SIBs cannot elect to issue non bail-in
unsecured senior debt
Excluded Liabilities. Bank customers' deposits including chequing accounts, savings accounts and term
deposits such as guaranteed investment certificates ("GICs"), secured liabilities (e.g., covered bonds), ABS
or most structured notes
All in scope liabilities, including those governed by foreign law, are subject to conversion and must indicate
in their contractual terms that the holder of the liability is bound by the application of the CDIC Act
Bail-in Conversion Terms
Flexible Conversion Terms. CDIC has discretion in determining the proportion of bail-in debt that is converted, as well as an appropriate conversion multiplier² which
respects the creditor hierarchy and that is more favourable than the multiplier provided to NVCC capital investors
No Contractual Trigger. Bail-in conversion is subject to regulatory determination of non-viability, not a fixed trigger
Full NVCC Conversion. There must be a full conversion of NVCC capital instruments before bail-in debt can be converted. Through other resolution tools, holders of
legacy non-NVCC capital instruments could also be subject to losses, resulting in bail-in note holders being better off than such junior-ranking instruments
No Creditor Worse Off. CDIC will compensate investors if they incur greater losses under bail-in than under a liquidation scenario. Bail-in debt holders rank pari
passu with other senior unsecured obligations, including deposits, for the purposes of the liquidation calculation
Equity Conversion. Unlike some other jurisdictions, bail-in is affected through equity conversion only, with no write-down option
Step 1:
Pre-loss balance
sheet
Step 2:
Losses diminish
assets and equity
Step 3:
Convert NVCC then,
if applicable, bail-in
debt into equity
40
— TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026

### Slide 42

**Text**

Limited Recourse Capital Notes (LRCNs)
LRCN Overview
LRCN holders’ interests rank equally with other LRCNs and Preferred Shares and are senior to common shares. LRCNs are issued only to institutional investors with no trading restrictions within the U.S. nor, after 4 months, within Canada
LRCN's qualify as AT1 capital, while being tax deductible for banks. LRCNs are not currently subject to withholding tax and, if it were levied in the future, the Bank would pay additional gross-up amounts to make holders whole (LRCNs only, not on recourse assets), subject to certain exceptions.
Limited Recourse: Upon a Recourse Event, investors in LRCNs have recourse only to the assets held in the Trust, initially Preferred Shares1; TD can also exchange the Preferred Shares into AT1 perpetual debt, subject to OSFI approval
Recourse Events are defined as follows:
1. Non-payment in cash of interest (5 business day cure right)
2. Non-payment in cash of the principal on the maturity date
3. Non-payment of redemption proceeds in cash
4. Event of Default (bankruptcy, insolvency or liquidation)
5. A Trigger Event2
LRCN Structure
1 TD (Issuer)
TD issues LRCNs to investors and receives proceeds in return
Coupon payments are paid by TD, generated through internal cash flow
2 Limited Recourse Trust (Trust)
The Trust is established by TD and acquires Non-Cumulative 5-Year NVCC Fixed Rate Reset Preferred Shares from TD ("LRCN Preferred Shares")
Upon a Recourse Event, the Limited Recourse assets held in the Trust are delivered to investors
The dividend rate (including reset spread and benchmark reference) and payment frequency on the LRCN Preferred Shares match LRCNs
Diagram: 1 Proceeds LRCNs LRCN Investors TD (Issuer) Preferred Shares 2 Trust
41 — TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026

The TD logo sits at top right, the slide number and footer text sit at bottom left, all text left-aligned except the centred section bar labels.

### Slide 43

**Review notes**

- What would change the score: I would split or trim the densest pages (slides 12, 36, 39, 41, 43) and move the long forward-looking text on slide 2 and the endnotes on slides 49 to 54 into a handout, with the first slide's empty lower area and the sparse slide 55 tidied.

**Text**

Non-Viability Contingent Capital (NVCC) / Credit hierarchy is codified as a principle in regulatory and legislative documents in Canada / Point of Non-Viability trigger occurs when OSFI determines the bank is no longer viable or if the bank accepts a government capital injection / – Importantly, liquidity assistance would not automatically constitute a non-viability trigger / Canadian NVCC notes are well-aligned to familiar features in international comparables: / – No incentives to redeem permitted (i.e., no step up of coupon rate/spread) / – Minimum term of at least 5 years; may be callable after 5 years / – Capital treatment is straight-line amortized in the final 5 years prior to maturity / In March 2023, OSFI issued this statement illustrating regulatory intent of the resolution regime in Canada: / If a deposit-taking bank reaches the point of non-viability, OSFI’s capital guidelines require Additional Tier 1 and Tier 2 capital instruments to be converted into common shares in a manner that respects the hierarchy of claims in liquidation. This results in significant dilution to existing common shareholders. Such a conversion ensures that Additional Tier 1 and Tier 2 holders are entitled to a more favorable economic outcome than existing common shareholders who would be the first to suffer losses.1 / NVCC Loss Absorption Jurisdictional Comparison2 / :: / Canada / Switzerland / EU / UK / US / Australia / Regulator / OSFI / FINMA / SRB & SSM / PRA / FDIC / APRA / Loss absorption trigger / NVCC Trigger Event / CET1 Trigger Event & Non-Viability Event / CET1 Trigger Event & Non-Viability Event / CET1 Trigger Event & Non-Viability Event / - / CET1 & Non-Viability Trigger Event / CET1 trigger / - / 7% high trigger / 5.125% low trigger / 5.125% / 7% differs by jurisdiction / 7% / - / 5.13% / Point of non-viability trigger / Contractual at PONV, at regulator's discretion. / Bail-in regulations provide that NVCC instruments should be converted ahead of, or at the same time as, bail-in liabilities / Contractual at PONV, at regulator's discretion Statutory regulations provide for write down / conversion, before or together with resolution power / Statutory at PONV, before or together with resolution power / Statutory at PONV, before or together with resolution power / Statutory, at regulator’s discretion / Contractual at PONV, at regulator's discretion / AT1 Discretionary Cancellation of Interest / Yes (for LRCNs, full discretion to trigger delivery of preferred shares in lieu of interest payments) / Yes (+ dividend stopper) / Yes / Yes / Yes (+ dividend stopper) / N/A APRA plans to phase out AT13 / Loss absorption mechanism / Conversion / AT1: Conversion or permanent write-down. No T2 issued / Conversion or temporary write-down / Conversion / Permanent write-down / AT1: Conversion T2: Conversion or permanent write down, if conversion fails / 42 — TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026

One table: columns are row-label column (::), Canada, Switzerland, EU, UK, US, Australia. Rows: header row; Regulator (OSFI, FINMA, SRB & SSM, PRA, FDIC, APRA); Loss absorption trigger; CET1 trigger; Point of non-viability trigger; AT1 Discretionary Cancellation of Interest; Loss absorption mechanism. Cell values are listed in the table element content. One quotation callout box in green with italic quotation text. One TD logo, top right. No charts, photographs or illustrations.

— TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026

**Layout**

Two-column grid of tables above and a 2x2 grid of line charts below. The title sits top-left at about 12.6% from the top, with the subtitle beneath it, left edge at about 3.5% of width. The TD logo sits at the top-right corner. The left table occupies the left half and the right table the right half, each under a dark-green header bar; the bullet line sits under the left table. Chart titles are left-aligned above each chart, with the Canadian Overnight Repo and U.S. SOFR charts in the left column and the CAD 5-Year and U.S. 7-Year charts in the right column. A grey note runs full width at about 90.8% from the top, with the page number and footer line at the bottom-left at about 92.7% from the top. Margins are about 3% on the left and about 4% on the right.

**Visuals**

Four line charts and two numeric tables. Chart 1: line chart titled 'Canadian Overnight Repo Rate Average (%)', x-axis monthly from Jan-25 to Jan-26 with labels every two months (Jan-25, Mar-25, May-25, Jul-25, Sep-25, Nov-25, Jan-26), y-axis labelled 3.10 and 2.10 only, single series in green #008A00 about 2.5 px thick. The line starts about 3.2 in Jan-25, steps down in stages (about 3.0 by Mar-25, about 2.9 by Sep-25, about 2.5 by Nov-25), and ends at 2.30% (printed label) at the right end, with the trough about 2.2 in Dec-25 to Jan-26. Chart 2: line chart titled 'CAD 5-Year Swap Rate (%)', x-axis monthly from Jan-21 to Jan-26 labelled Jan-21, Jun-21, Nov-21, Apr-22, Sep-22, Feb-23, Jul-23, Dec-23, May-24, Oct-24, Mar-25, Aug-25, Jan-26; y-axis 0.00 to 5.00 in steps of 1.00. Series rises from about 0.8 in Jan-21 to about 4.8 peak near Dec-23, falls to about 2.5 by Oct-24 and ends at 2.78% (printed label). Chart 3: line chart titled 'U.S. Secured Overnight Financing Rate (%)', x-axis Jan-25 to Jan-26 with the same bimonthly labels, y-axis labelled 3.10, 4.10 and 5.10. Series is flat near about 4.3 until Sep-25, then steps down to about 3.7 by Jan-26, ending at 3.68% (printed label). Chart 4: line chart titled 'U.S. 7-Year Swap Rate (%)', x-axis Jan-19 to Jan-26 labelled at each January, y-axis 0.00 to 5.00 in steps of 1.00. Series starts about 2.3 in Jan-19, falls to about 0.1 around Jan-20, rises to about 4.5 around Jan-24, and ends at 3.61% (printed label). No legend on any chart; each chart has a single series with no markers. Table A: 'NII impact from 25 bps change in short-term interest rates1' in a dark-green #006700 header bar with white bold text. Columns: row labels, Q1'26 (+25bps, -25bps), Q4'25 (+25bps, -25bps). Rows: CAD $52, ($52), $54, ($55); USD $39, ($40), $40, ($40); Total $91, ($92), $94, ($95) in bold. Table B: 'NII impact from 100 bps change in interest rates across the curve1' in the same bar style. Columns: Q1'26 (+100bps, -100bps), Q4'25 (+100bps, -100bps). Rows: CAD $382, ($417), $400, ($441); USD $363, ($398), $390, ($419); Total $745, ($815), $790, ($860) in bold. Both tables use thin green horizontal rules under the headers and between the rows. Footnote superscript 1 follows each header.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF covering the whole slide. White background with green accents. Title in Arial Bold, green #008A00, very large (about 5.2% of slide height, capital-led). Subtitle in Arial Bold, green #008A00, about 3.7% of slide height. Table header bars are solid #006700 with white bold text about 2.0% of slide height. Column and sub-headers are bold dark green #03522C about 2.0%. Body numbers are regular Arial in #03522C about 2.0%; totals bold. Chart titles are Arial Bold in #03522C about 2.4% of slide height. Axis labels are Arial regular in #03522C about 1.8%. Data labels at line ends are Arial Bold in #008A00 about 1.5%. Lines are #008A00 about 2.5 px thick with no fill. Table rules are thin green lines. Note text is Arial regular grey #7F7F7F about 1.3%. Footer text is Arial regular black #000000 about 1.5%, page number #1C1C1C. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

- shape at x 0, y 0, w 100, h 100 [#FFFFFF]: Full-slide white background rectangle (Covers the slide; no outline.)

**Layout**

A single left-aligned title block sits in the lower-middle of the slide, starting at about 5.2% from the left edge. A full-width green horizontal rule runs from the left margin to the right edge at about 53.5% of the slide height, just above the title. The TD logo sits in the top-right corner, and a small footer with the page number and deck title sits at the bottom left, about 92.8% down the slide. Nothing else is placed on the slide.

Page number in near-black #1C1C1C; footer text in black #000000. Typeface is Arial (sans-serif): 'Appendix' in Arial Bold, 'Economic Outlook' in Arial Regular, both in green. The title is about 10% of slide height and the subtitle about 8.2%. Footer text is about 1.5% of slide height. Rule is a plain 2px-equivalent solid line with square ends, no shadow or rounding.

### Slide 46

**Text**

Interest Rate Outlook1 / Interest Rates, Canada and U.S. / (%) / 6 / 5 / 4 / 3 / 2 / 1 / 0 / 2001 / 2006 / 2011 / 2016 / 2021 / 2026 / Forecast / U.S. Federal / Funds Rate / Bank of / Canada / Overnight / Policy Rate / The Federal Reserve (Fed) reduced the target for / the federal funds rate to 3.5%-3.75% in December / 2025 and held it steady in January 2026. We expect / the Fed to deliver two more cuts beginning in mid- / 2026. / The Bank of Canada (BoC) held the overnight rate / steady at 2.25% in December and January. We / anticipate the current policy setting to be maintained / for the remainder of this year. / By the end of 2026, we expect the Federal Reserve / will have reduced its policy rate to 3.25% and the / Bank of Canada to hold at 2.25%. / 45 / — TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026

**Layout**

Single-slide layout on a white field with a left chart column (about x 3 to 52) and a right text column (about x 59 to 97) separated by a thin vertical grey divider at about x 54.4 running from about y 18 to y 91. The title sits top-left at about x 3.5, y 12.6, with the chart subtitle beneath it at about x 6.8, y 20.9. Right-column bullets are left-aligned with a green highlight box below them at about x 58.8, y 69.5. The TD logo sits top-right at about x 95, y 3.9. The footer with page number 45 and the source line sits bottom-left at about y 92.7, and margins are roughly 3.5 percent on the left.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled Interest Rates, Canada and U.S. with y-axis labelled (%) ranging 0 to 6 in steps of 1 and x-axis years labelled 2001, 2006, 2011, 2016, 2021, 2026 with a dashed vertical forecast line at about 2025.8 labelled Forecast. Series 1 is the U.S. Federal Funds Rate drawn as a thick dark green (#1E3A24) line; about values: 2001 5.0, 2002 1.75, 2003 1.0, 2004 1.0, 2006 5.25, 2007 5.25, 2008 0.25, 2009 to 2015 about 0.25, 2016 about 0.5, 2018 about 2.5, 2019 about 2.5, 2020 about 0.25 through 2022, 2023 about 5.5, 2024 about 4.5, 2025 about 3.75, 2026 about 3.25. Series 2 is the Bank of Canada overnight rate drawn as a thick bright green (#00A651) line; about values: 2001 5.0, 2002 2.0, 2003 3.25, 2004 2.0, 2005 2.5, 2006 4.25, 2007 4.5, 2008 3.0, 2009 to 2010 about 0.25, 2011 to 2015 about 1.0, 2016 about 0.5, 2017 about 0.75, 2018 to 2019 about 1.75, 2020 to 2022 about 0.25, 2023 about 5.0, 2024 about 3.25, 2025 about 2.25, 2026 about 2.25. No legend; the series are identified by callouts. Callout Bank of Canada Overnight Policy Rate in italic black text with a thin black arrow pointing down to the bright green line at about 2012. Callout U.S. Federal Funds Rate in italic black text with a thin black arrow pointing down to the dark green peak at about 2019. Axis lines are black, about 0.1 percent of slide width. No highlighted points other than the two callouts. Below the chart area, a green square logo sits top-right containing white TD letters.

### Slide 47

**Text**

TD Economics Update 1 / Global Outlook: Strong start to 2025 followed by slowing growth / Global growth is expected to slow in 2026, as decelerating cyclical momentum is being reinforced by higher tariffs. / The slowdown in global growth is largely driven by slowing growth in Asia, especially the fast-growing export-oriented emerging market economies that are affected by U.S. tariffs. / Past central bank rate cuts will continue to support growth, but that impact is likely to wane for most developed market economies as central banks there are largely done cutting rates, and some are likely to be raising rates this year. A number of major developing market central banks may still be cutting interest rates this year, such as Brazil, India, and Mexico. / U.S. economy to remain strong in 2026 / U.S. economic growth is expected to expand by 2.8% in 2026, an acceleration from 2025's 2.2%. / The U.S. economy has entered 2026 with considerable momentum, buoyed by a resilient consumer and sustained investments in AI. While headwinds to growth from tariffs and trade uncertainty remain, its effects are likely to be eclipsed by fiscal tailwinds from the One Big Beautiful Bill Act, easier financial conditions, a lighter touch on regulation and further investments in AI. / With the labour market stabilizing in recent months, and inflationary pressure still elevated, the Federal Reserve is likely to keep the policy rate unchanged at its current target range of 3.5%-3.75% through mid-year. We expect two additional quarter-point rate cuts by year-end. / Canadian economy struggles under high U.S. tariffs / Trade uncertainty persists and is expected to continue to weigh on the economy. Growth is anticipated to register 1.1% in 2026 and 1.8% in 2027. Strong job gains in recent months have helped push the unemployment rate lower, reaching 6.5% in January. Employment growth is expected to slow, but the unemployment rate is forecast to remain relatively steady due to weak labour force growth. / Headline consumer price inflation was 2.3% (y/y) in January, while the Bank of Canada's core measures of inflation cooled to an average of 2.5%. With economic uncertainty still high, and inflation on a downward path, we expect the Bank of Canada to leave interest rates unchanged for the remainder of the year. / 46 / — TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026

**Layout**

Single full-width text column with a title at top left and three full-width section header bars stacked down the slide, each followed by square-bulleted paragraphs. The title sits at about 12.6% from the top at a left margin of 3.5%, and the TD logo sits in the top right corner at about 95% from the left. Body text is left-aligned under a hanging bullet indent at 3.9% and runs to about 92% of the width. The footer carries the page number 46 and the text TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026 at the bottom left, around 92.7% from the top.

**Visuals**

Corner logo: the TD bank logo, white stylised TD letters on a bright green square (about #00B200) at top right, 42 by 37 pixels. No charts, photographs, illustrations or diagrams. The three dark green full-width bars are plain filled rectangles used as section headers, not visuals.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF covering the full slide. Plain white background with no gradient or texture. Title is bold sans-serif (Arial Bold) in green #008A00 at about 5.2% of slide height, with a superscript 1 at about 3.5%. Section header bars are filled #03522C with bold white (#FFFFFF) Arial headings at about 2.6% of slide height, centred. Body text is regular Arial in dark green #03522C at about 2.1% of slide height, with square Wingdings bullets in #03522C. Footer is Arial at about 1.5% of slide height: page number in #1C1C1C and the footer text in #000000. Shapes are sharp-cornered rectangles with no shadows, borders or gradients.

- text at x 5.4, y 53.4, w 86.3, h 2.1 [ArialMT; font size 2.13; #03522C]: and trade uncertainty remain, its effects are likely to be eclipsed by fiscal tailwinds from the One Big Beautiful Bill Act, easier financial conditions, a lighter touch on
- text at x 32.2, y 66.3, w 35.9, h 2.6 [Arial-BoldMT; font size 2.60; #FFFFFF]: Canadian economy struggles under high U.S. tariffs​

Endnotes on Slides 3 to 6 / Slide 3 / 1. Deposits based on total of average personal and business deposits during the quarter. U.S. Banking includes Schwab Insured Deposit Accounts (IDAs). / 2. Users who have logged in via their mobile device at least once in the last 90 days. / Slide 4 / 1. Canadian Peers defined as other 4 big banks; Royal Bank of Canada (RY), Bank of Montreal (BMO), The Bank of Nova Scotia (BNS) and Canadian Imperial Bank of Commerce (CM). / 2. North American Peers – defined as Canadian Peers and U.S. Peers. U.S. Peers defined as Citigroup Inc., Bank of America Corporation, JPMorgan Chase & Co., Wells Fargo & Company and U.S. Bancorp. / 3. The Bank prepares its Interim Consolidated Financial Statements in accordance with International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS), the current generally accepted accounting principles (GAAP), and refers to results prepared in accordance / with IFRS as the “reported” results. The Bank also utilizes non-GAAP financial measures such as “adjusted” results (i.e., reported results excluding "items of note") and non-GAAP ratios to assess each of its businesses and to measure overall / Bank performance. The Bank believes that non-GAAP financial measures and non-GAAP ratios provide the reader with a better understanding of how management views the Bank’s performance. Non-GAAP financial measures and non-GAAP / ratios used in this presentation are not defined terms under IFRS and, therefore, may not be comparable to similar terms used by other issuers. Refer to the "Significant Events", How We Performed" or "How Our Businesses Performed" sections / of the Bank's Q1 2026 MD&A (available at https://www.td.com/investor-relations and www.sedarplus.ca), which is incorporated by reference, for further explanation, a list of the items of note, and a reconciliation of adjusted to reported results. / 4. This measure has been calculated in accordance with OSFI's Capital Adequacy Requirements guideline. / Slide 5 / 1. For the purpose of calculating contribution by each business segment, earnings from the Corporate segment are excluded. Numbers may not add to 100% due to rounding. / Slide 6 / 1. Please refer to Slide 3, Endnote 4 / 2. Please refer to Slide 4, Endnote 3. / 3. The Bank's fiscal 2026 and medium-term financial targets are based on forward-looking assumptions that have inherent risks and uncertainties. Results may vary depending on actual economic conditions, including the level of unemployment, / interest rates, and economic growth or contraction, the operating environment, including regulatory requirements, political environment, and competitive landscape, and the Bank's assumptions on future business performance, including credit / conditions and performance, inclusive of policy and trade uncertainty and borrower or industry specific credit factors and conditions, and foreign exchange impact. These assumptions are subject to inherent uncertainties and may vary based on / factors outside the Bank’s control, including those set out at the beginning of this presentation. For additional information about risks and uncertainties that may impact the Bank's estimates refer to Slide 1 of this presentation and in the “Risk / Factors That May Affect Future Results” section of the Bank's Q1 2026 MD&A. / 4. Pre-tax, pre-provision earnings (PTPP) is a non-GAAP financial measure that is typically calculated by subtracting expenses from revenues. At the total Bank level, TD calculates PTPP as the difference between adjusted revenue (for U.S. / Banking in US$) net of insurance service expense (ISE), and adjusted expenses (for U.S. Banking in US$), grossed up by the retailer program partners' share of PCL for the Bank's U.S. strategic card portfolio. Collectively, these adjustments / provide a measure of PTPP that management believes is more reflective of underlying business performance. / 5. Operating Leverage is a non-GAAP measure that the Bank calculates as the difference between the percentage change in adjusted revenue (U.S. Banking in US$) net of insurance service expense, and adjusted expenses (U.S. Banking in US$) / grossed up by the retailer program partners’ share of PCL for the Bank’s U.S. strategic card portfolio. Collectively, these adjustments provide a measure of operating leverage that management believes is more reflective of underlying business / performance. / 6. Efficiency ratio (net of ISE) is a non-GAAP ratio. The adjustment of insurance service expense (ISE) provides a measure of efficiency that management believes is more reflective of underlying business performance. / 7. TD calculates adjusted organic capital accretion as adjusted net income after-tax less dividends less risk-weighted assets (RWA) growth (excluding Model / Methodology updates and FX), which is converted to basis points (bps) impact to / Common Equity Tier 1 (CET1) capital. Capital Accretion measures performance of generating incremental capital.

- text at x 3.4, y 77.2, w 0.7, h 1.5 [ArialMT; font size 1.49; #008A00]: 6.

- text at x 5.3, y 80.8, w 42.1, h 1.5 [ArialMT; font size 1.49; #1C1C1C]: Common Equity Tier 1 (CET1) capital. Capital Accretion measures performance of generating incremental capital.

### Slide 50

**Review notes**

- What would change the score: I would split or trim the densest pages (slides 12, 36, 39, 41, 43) and move the long forward-looking text on slide 2 and the endnotes on slides 49 to 54 into a handout, with the first slide's empty lower area and the sparse slide 55 tidied.

**Text**

Endnotes on Slides 11 to 13 / Slide 11 / 1. Please refer to Slide 4, Endnote 3. / 2. Please refer to Slide 3, Endnote 4. / 3. Please refer to Slide 6, Endnote 3. / 4. In estimating its expense growth expectations, the Bank has assumed that the following three factors on the Bank’s fiscal 2026 adjusted expenses will be the same as the Bank’s fiscal 2025 adjusted expenses: (i) variable compensation in / Wholesale Banking and Wealth Management, (ii) foreign exchange translation, and (iii) gross-up of the retailer program partners’ share of PCL for the Bank’s U.S. strategic card portfolio. The Bank’s assumptions are subject to inherent / uncertainties and may vary based on factors both within and outside the Bank’s control, including the accuracy of the Bank’s employee compensation and benefit expense forecasts, impact of business performance on variable compensation, / inflation, the pace of productivity initiatives across the organization, and unexpected expenses such as legal matters. For additional information about risks and uncertainties that may impact the Bank’s estimates refer to Slide 1 of this / presentation and in the “Risk Factors That May Affect Future Results” section of the Bank’s Q1 2026 MD&A. / 5. The Bank’s estimated PCL range is based on forward-looking assumptions that have inherent risks and uncertainties. Results may vary depending on actual economic or credit conditions and performance, such as the level of unemployment, / interest rates, economic growth or contraction, and borrower or industry specific credit factors and conditions, inclusive of policy and trade uncertainty. The Bank's PCL estimate is subject to risks and uncertainties including those set out on Slide / 1 of this presentation and in the "Risk Factors That May Affect Future Results" section of the Q1 2026 MD&A. / 6. PCL Ratio: Provision for Credit Losses on a quarterly annualized basis/Average Net Loans. / Slide 12 / 1. The Bank's expectations regarding the restructuring program are subject to inherent uncertainties and are based on the Bank's assumptions regarding certain factors, including rate of natural attrition, talent re-deployment opportunities, years-of- / service, execution timing of actions, and foreign exchange impacts. For additional information about risks and uncertainties that may impact the Bank's estimates refer to Slide 1 of this presentation and in the “Risk Factors That May Affect Future / Results” section of the Bank’s Q1 2026 MD&A. / 2. The Bank's expectations regarding cost savings in fiscal 2026 and annualized cost savings over the medium-term are based on the Bank's forward-looking assumptions, including general economic conditions, foreign exchange impact, and the / operating environment, including regulatory, legal and political landscape. These assumptions are subject to inherent uncertainties and may vary based on factors outside the Bank’s control, including those set out at the beginning of this / presentation. Refer to the “Risk Factors That May Affect Future Results” section of the Bank’s Q1 2026 MD&A for additional information about risks and uncertainties that may impact the Bank’s estimates. / Slide 13 / 1. As previously disclosed in the Bank’s 2024 MD&A, on October 10, 2024, the Bank announced that, following active cooperation and engagement with authorities and regulators, it reached a resolution (the "Global Resolution") of previously / disclosed investigations related to its U.S. Bank Secrecy Act ("BSA") and Anti-Money Laundering ("AML") compliance programs (collectively, the "U.S. BSA/AML program). The Bank and certain of its U.S. subsidiaries consented to orders with / the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC), the Federal Reserve Board (FRB), and the Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) and entered into plea agreements with the Department of Justice (DOJ), Criminal Division, Money / Laundering and Asset Recovery Section and the United States Attorney’s Office for the District of New Jersey. The Bank is focused on meeting the terms of the consent orders and plea agreements, including meeting its requirements to / remediate the Bank’s U.S. BSA/AML program. In addition, the Bank is also undertaking remediation of the Bank’s enterprise-wide AML/Anti-Terrorist Financing and Sanctions Programs (“Enterprise AML Program”). For additional information on / the Global Resolution, the Bank's U.S. BSA/AML program remediation activities, the Bank’s Enterprise AML Program improvement activities, and the risks associated with the foregoing, see Slide 1 of this presentation and the “Update on the / Remediation of the U.S. BSA/AML Program and Enterprise AML Program” and “Risk Factors That May Affect Future Results – Remediation of the Bank's U.S.BS/AML Program and Enterprise AML Program” sections of the Bank’s 2025 MD&A / and "Update on the remediation of the U.S. Bank Secrecy Act (BSA)/Anti-Money Laundering (AML) Program and Enterprise AML Program" section of the Bank's Q1 2026 MD&A. / 49 / — TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026

**Layout**

Text-only endnotes page on a white field with a single left-aligned column starting at about 3.4% from the left edge. The title sits top-left at about 5.4% from the top, with the TD logo boxed at the top-right corner; three numbered endnote groups (Slide 11, Slide 12, Slide 13) stack vertically with bold green group headings. The footer with page number 49 and the TD investor presentation label sits bottom-left at about 92.7% from the top.

**Visuals**

The only non-text visual is the TD logo at top right: a solid green square (#00A000-range green) about 3.3% of slide width, with the white letters TD in a bold geometric sans-serif, set in a sharp-cornered box. No photographs, charts, tables or illustrations are present.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradients or shadows. Title is bold sans-serif (Arial Bold style) in green #008A00, about 5.2% of slide height. Group headings are Arial Bold in green #008A00 at about 1.5% of slide height. Numbers are green #008A00 and endnote body text is Arial regular in near-black #1C1C1C at about 1.5% of slide height. Footer text is Arial regular in #1C1C1C and #000000. Lines are unruled and there are no shapes beyond the logo; the page is dense with generous white space at the bottom.

Endnotes on Slides 22 to 30
Slide 22
1. GIL Ratio: Gross Impaired Loans/Gross Loans & Acceptances (both are spot) by portfolio.
Slide 23
1. PCL Ratio: Provision for Credit Losses on a quarterly annualized basis/Average Net Loans & Acceptances.
2. Net Total Bank and U.S. Banking PCL ratios exclude credit losses associated with the retailer program partners' share of the U.S. Strategic Cards Portfolio, which is recorded in the Corporate Segment.
3. Gross Total Bank, U.S. Banking & Corporate PCL ratios include the retailer program partners' share of the U.S. Strategic Cards Portfolio, which is recorded in the Corporate Segment.
4. Please refer to Slide 4, Endnote 3.
Slide 24
1. PCL-impaired represents Stage 3 PCL under IFRS 9, performing represents Stage 1 and Stage 2 on financial assets, loan commitments, and financial guarantees.
Slide 25
1. Stage 3 provision for (recovery of) credit losses (impaired) as a % of Average Net Loans and Acceptances, on a quarterly annualized basis. Primarily based on the geographic location responsible for recording the transaction. International not shown. Includes loans that are measured at FVOCI. Includes provision for off-balance sheet instruments.
Slide 26
1. Excludes Wealth Management & Insurance segment and Wholesale mortgage portfolio.
2. RESL Portfolio Current Loan to Value is calculated with the Teranet-National Bank House Price Index™ and weighted by the total exposure, based on outstanding mortgage balance and/or the HELOC authorized credit limit for both insured and uninsured exposures, excluding the Wholesale mortgage portfolio. 3. The territories are included as follows: Yukon is included in British Columbia; Nunavut is included in Ontario; and Northwest Territories is included in the Prairies region.
Slide 27

**Layout**

**Text**

Endnotes on Slides 31 to 37
Slide 32
1. Capital and liquidity measures are calculated in accordance with OSFI's Capital Adequacy Requirements, Leverage Requirements, and Liquidity Adequacy Requirements guidelines.
2. Please refer to Slide 4, Endnote 3.
3. Numbers may not add due to rounding.
Slide 33
1. Please refer to Slide 32, Endnote 1.
2. Reflects debt outstanding as at, and converted at FX rate as at January 31, 2026. Numbers may not add due to rounding.
3. These measures have been calculated in accordance with OSFI's Total Loss Absorbing Capacity (TLAC) guideline.
4. Includes par value of outstanding senior unsecured long-term debt issued on or after September 23, 2018, with a remaining term to maturity of greater than 1 year. Senior unsecured long-term debt with original term to maturity less than 400 days will not be eligible for bail-in and would not qualify as TLAC.
Slide 34
1. Business deposits exclude wholesale funding.
2. As measured by OSFI market share data.
3. Based on total assets. Excludes Goldman Sachs and Morgan Stanley. Source: S&P Global Market Intelligence.

5. Liquidity measures are calculated in accordance with OSFI's Liquidity Adequacy Requirements guidelines.
Slide 35
1. As of January 31, 2026. Credit ratings are not recommendations to purchase, sell, or hold a financial obligation in as much as they do not comment on market price or suitability for a particular investor. Ratings are subject to revision or withdrawal at any time by the rating organization.
2. Subject to conversion under the bank recapitalization "bail-in" regime.
3. Ratings reflect holding company senior unsecured ratings.
4. Please refer to Slide 4, Endnote 1.
5. Please refer to Slide 4, Endnote 2.
Slide 36
1. For wholesale term debt that has bullet maturities.
2. Based on first par redemption date. The timing of an actual redemption is subject to management’s view at the time as well as applicable regulatory and corporate governance approvals.

Slide 37
1. Excludes certain private placement and structured notes.
2. In Canadian dollars equivalent with exchange rate as at January 31, 2026.
3. Represents mortgage-backed securities issued to external investors only.
4. Includes Limited Recourse Capital Notes, Preferred Shares, Subordinated Debt and AT1 Perpetual Debt. Subordinated debt includes certain private placement notes. These instruments are not considered wholesale funding as they may be raised primarily for capital management purposes.
52 — TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026

**Layout**

Single white slide with a title at top left and a TD logo at top right in the corner. Below the title, six numbered endnote blocks run in one left-aligned column starting at about 3.4% of slide width, each opening with a bold green slide heading and numbered items with hanging indents; the column leaves the lower third empty. The footer sits bottom left with the page number 52 followed by an em dash and the document name, and there is no slide number beyond that printed page number.

**Visuals**

None apart from the corner logo: a green square TD logo with white 'TD' letterforms at top right. No charts, tables, photographs or illustrations.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF covering the full slide. Plain white background with no gradients, shadows or decorative shapes. Title in Arial Bold, green #008A00, about 5.2% of slide height. Endnote headings in Arial Bold green #008A00, body in Arial regular #1C1C1C, both about 1.5% of slide height. Numbers in the numbered lists are green #008A00 in regular Arial. Footer page number and text in Arial regular, #1C1C1C and #000000 respectively, about 1.5% of slide height. One stray period after 'guidelines' in Slide 34 item 5 is set in blue #0000FF.

**Text**

Endnotes on Slides 39 to 46
Slide 39
1.
Current Loan to Value is calculated with the Teranet-National Bank House Price Index and weighted by balance.

Please refer to Slide 35, Endnote 1.

Slide 40
1.
Any non-NVCC preferred shares and non-NVCC subordinated debt issued on or after September 23, 2018 would also be in scope.
2.
In determining the multiplier, CDIC must take into consideration the requirement in the Bank Act for banks to maintain adequate capital and that equally ranking bail-in eligible instruments must be converted in the same proportion and receive the
same number of common shares per dollar of claim.
Slide 41
1.
Initially, the assets held in the Trust will consist of the series of Preferred Shares issued in connection with each LRCN series. Following the issuance of the LRCNs, the assets held in the Trust may also consist of (i) common shares issued upon
a Trigger Event, (ii) cash from the redemption, or the purchase by the Bank for cancellation, of the Preferred Share series, or (iii) any combination thereof, depending on the circumstances.
2.
Under the OSFI Guideline for Capital Adequacy Requirements (CAR), Chapter 2 ‒ Definition of Capital, effective November 2024, each of the following constitutes a Trigger Event: (i) the Superintendent publicly announces that the
Superintendent is of the opinion that the Bank has ceased, or is about to cease, to be viable and that, after the conversion or write-off, as applicable, of all contingent instruments and taking into account any other factors or circumstances that are
considered relevant or appropriate, it is reasonably likely that the viability of the Bank will be restored or maintained; or (ii) the federal or a provincial government in Canada publicly announces that the Bank has accepted or agreed to accept a
capital injection, or equivalent support, from the federal government or any provincial government without which the Bank would have been determined by the Superintendent to be non-viable.
Slide 42
1.
Link to full OSFI's statement: https://www.osfi-bsif.gc.ca/en/news/osfi-reinforces-guidance-additional-tier-1-tier-2-capital-instruments.
2.
This comparison table is provided for illustrative purposes and is meant to highlight differences in market practice. Information has been sourced from publicly available information.
3.
The Australian Prudential Regulation Authority (APRA) announced on December 9, 2024 its intention to phase out AT1 capital instruments from Australian banks beginning January 1, 2027. APRA will allow existing AT1 instruments to count as
Tier 2 until their first scheduled call date occurring by 2032.
Slide 43
1.
Numbers may not add due to rounding.
Slide 45
1.
TD Economics, February 2026. For recent economic analysis and research please refer to https://economics.td.com.
Slide 46
1.
Please refer to Slide 45, Endnote 1.

**Layout**

Single left-aligned text column starting under the title at the top left, with generous left margin of about 2.6% of width; each endnote block is a bold green slide heading followed by green numbered items with hanging indents. TD logo sits in the top right corner. Footer with page number at bottom left, followed by a dash and the footer text; bottom third of the slide is empty.

**Visuals**

Logo: the TD bank logo, white stylised 'TD' letters on a solid green square (about #00A300), placed at the top right, 42 by 37 pixels. No charts, photographs or other graphics.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background. Title in bold green Arial-style sans-serif at about 5.2% of slide height (cap height about 3.6%). Body text in regular Arial-style sans-serif at about 1.5% of slide height, dark grey #1C1C1C. Slide headings and numerals in bold/regular green #008A00. Footer text in regular black #000000 at about 1.5%. Flat, no shadows, gradients, rules or shapes.

### Slide 55

**Review notes**

- What would change the score: I would split or trim the densest pages (slides 12, 36, 39, 41, 43) and move the long forward-looking text on slide 2 and the endnotes on slides 49 to 54 into a handout, with the first slide's empty lower area and the sparse slide 55 tidied.

**Text**

Investor Relations Contacts / Phone: / (416) 308-9030 or 1 (866) 486-4826 / Email: / tdir@td.com / Website: / www.td.com/investor / 54 / — TD INVESTOR PRESENTATION Q1 2026

**Layout**

Single-column text block flush left at the top, with the title at top-left above the contact list and the TD logo at top-right corner. Contact labels and values stack in alternating rows with the footer page number and running title at the bottom-left; the rest of the slide is empty white space.

**Visuals**

Non-text visual: a small square TD logo at top-right, bright green (#00A800-like) square with white stylised 'TD' letters. No charts, photos or diagrams.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradient or texture. Title in bold green (#008A00) sans-serif (Arial Bold), about 5.2% of slide height, flat with no shadow. Labels in bold dark grey (#37373A) Arial Bold about 2.0% of slide height; contact values in Arial regular, dark green (#006700) for phone and mid green (#58AA46) underlined for email and website. Footer in Arial regular about 1.5% of slide height, near-black (#1C1C1C/#000000). No shapes, lines or corner radii.
